The validity of a prediction set is determined by a user-defined binary admissibility function depending on the target application. For example, requiring at least one program in the set to pass all test cases in code generation application. To address this problem, we develop a simple and effective conformal inference algorithm referred to as Generative Prediction Sets (GPS). Given a set of calibration examples and black-box access to a deep generative model, GPS can generate prediction sets with provable guarantees. The key insight behind GPS is to exploit the inherent structure within the distribution over the minimum number of samples needed to obtain an admissible output to develop a simple conformal regression approach over the minimum number of samples. Experiments on multiple datasets for code and math word problems using different large language models demonstrate the efficacy of GPS over state-of-the-art methods.
- **Summary**: The paper introduces Generative Prediction Sets (GPS), a novel conformal inference algorithm designed to generate valid and small prediction sets from black-box deep generative models (DGMs). The approach reduces the problem to conformal regression by learning to predict the minimum number of samples needed to obtain an admissible output, leveraging inherent structure within the distribution of sample counts. GPS is evaluated on code generation and math word problems, demonstrating improved performance over existing methods like Conformal Language Modeling (CLM), especially in achieving lower abstention rates at tighter confidence levels while maintaining competitive set sizes. Key advantages highlighted include simplifying the CP problem, computational efficiency, and compatibility with batch inference pipelines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of reducing the problem of generating valid prediction sets for DGMs to a conformal regression problem on the minimum number of required samples has novelty. This approach avoids the enumeration or sequential analysis inherent in methods like CLM and provides an alternative means of theoretically sound uncertainty quantification for DGMs. The integration of an admissibility function to handle semantic equivalence of generated outputs is also a valuable addition.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the limitations of existing CP techniques for DGMs with combinatorial output spaces. The ability to generate valid prediction sets with guaranteed coverage provides a crucial safety mechanism for deploying DGMs in safety-critical domains, including software engineering and education. Lower abstention rates at tight confidence levels are a practically important result, leading to more useful uncertainty quantification. The algorithm's efficiency and ease of implementation contribute to its broader applicability.
    However, it is worth discussing the limitations of this work. First, this framework requires construction of admissibility estimator which determines three key performance metrics: empirical set sizes, sampling efficiency, and adaptivity of the prediction sets. Furthermore, since the approach is based on split conformal prediction, it suffers from efficiency issues due to split, as discussed in the paper. This could be mitigated by using more advanced methods such as cross-conformal prediction.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The reduction to conformal regression is elegant and effective.
    *   The experimental results clearly demonstrate the efficacy of GPS.
    *   The algorithm is well-motivated and addresses an important practical problem.
    *   The theoretical guarantees of coverage are a strong selling point.
    *   It's easy to implement and computationally more efficient than the baselines.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance of GPS depends on the admissibility estimator, which needs to be trained in each environment.
    *   The evaluation focuses on a limited set of DGMs and benchmarks and might not generalize to all DGMs and tasks.
    *   There could be a more comprehensive discussion of the limitations, such as the computational cost of training the admissibility estimator or potential sensitivity to the choice of features used in the auxiliary predictor.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work is likely to influence further research in uncertainty quantification for DGMs, especially within the CP framework. The proposed reduction technique might inspire similar approaches for other challenging settings. The reported improvements in abstention rates and set sizes also suggest potential for real-world deployment in applications where DGM outputs need to be accompanied by reliability metrics. It’ll inspire to include conditional coverage using conformal guarantees which will strengthen this framework even more.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** GPS offers a novel and practically useful solution to generating valid prediction sets for DGMs. While improvements in admissibility estimator are welcome for various kinds of datasets, it moves the field forward in a significant way and warrants a score of 7.5.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Probing LLMs for Multilingual Discourse Generalization Through a Unified Label Set
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10515v1)
- **Authors**: Florian Eichin, Yang Janet Liu, Barbara Plank, Michael A. Hedderich
- **Abstract**: Discourse understanding is essential for many NLP tasks, yet most existing work remains constrained by framework-dependent discourse representations. This work investigates whether large language models (LLMs) capture discourse knowledge that generalizes across languages and frameworks. We address this question along two dimensions: (1) developing a unified discourse relation label set to facilitate cross-lingual and cross-framework discourse analysis, and (2) probing LLMs to assess whether they encode generalizable discourse abstractions. Using multilingual discourse relation classification as a testbed, we examine a comprehensive set of 23 LLMs of varying sizes and multilingual capabilities. Our results show that LLMs, especially those with multilingual training corpora, can generalize discourse information across languages and frameworks. Further layer-wise analyses reveal that language generalization at the discourse level is most salient in the intermediate layers. Lastly, our error analysis provides an account of challenging relation classes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of large language models (LLMs) to generalize discourse understanding across languages and frameworks.  It tackles this by (1) creating a unified discourse relation label set to bridge different annotation schemes, and (2) probing a variety of LLMs to see if they encode generalizable discourse abstractions rather than dataset-specific biases. The authors use multilingual discourse relation classification as their benchmark task, analyzing 23 LLMs of varying sizes and multilingual capabilities.  They find that LLMs, especially those with extensive multilingual training, demonstrate the capacity to generalize discourse information across languages and frameworks. Layer-wise analysis suggests that the intermediate layers are most critical for language generalization at the discourse level.  The authors also provide an error analysis, highlighting challenges with certain relation classes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic exploration of cross-lingual and cross-framework discourse generalization in LLMs using a unified label set at a significant scale (23 LLMs, 13 languages). Prior work either focused on specific frameworks, individual languages, or smaller model scales. The creation of the unified label set itself represents a contribution, although building upon existing mapping proposals. The layer-wise analysis is also a valuable contribution for understanding how LLMs organize discourse information.

*   **Significance:** This work addresses a key limitation in discourse processing: the fragmentation of research and resources due to framework-specific annotations. By demonstrating LLMs' potential for generalizable discourse understanding, the paper opens up avenues for developing more robust and versatile discourse models.  The findings could influence future research directions in multilingual NLP and discourse analysis, potentially leading to better transfer learning and more broadly applicable discourse processing tools.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Experimentation:** The paper includes a thorough examination of a wide array of LLMs.
    *   **Unified Label Set:** The authors create a valuable resource for future research.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The layer-wise and error analyses provide insightful observations about LLMs' discourse understanding capabilities and limitations.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper identifies and addresses a crucial challenge in discourse processing (framework-dependent representations).
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors make their code available (according to the paper), enhancing reproducibility and allowing others to build on their findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Data Imbalance:** As the authors acknowledge, the DISRPT dataset has a significant language imbalance, with English dominating. This could bias the findings towards English-centric discourse patterns. The authors attempt to address this using specific probing, but the bias remains a limitation.
    *   **Simplified Label Set:** While the unified label set is valuable, the abstraction process may have oversimplified some discourse relations, potentially masking nuances captured by the original annotation schemes.
    *   **Probing Limitations:** Probing methods, while useful, can only provide indirect evidence about the information encoded in LLMs. It is possible that LLMs use different or more complex strategies to represent discourse.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis:** Although the error analysis is useful, it could be expanded to investigate errors on a per-language, per-framework basis to uncover more fine-grained differences.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could encourage the development of more linguistically diverse discourse datasets and annotation schemes. It could also inspire the use of LLMs in cross-lingual discourse analysis tasks and motivate further research into how LLMs represent and process discourse information.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a significant contribution to the field, although not a revolutionary one. It systematically investigates a key problem in discourse processing using a large-scale experimental setup, providing valuable insights and a useful unified label set. The limitations, particularly the data imbalance and the simplified label set, slightly reduce the overall impact.

Score: 7

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**
A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contributions, its comprehensiveness, and its clear implications for future research.  It isn't higher because of the limitations already outlined. The key is its systematic approach, which significantly advances the understanding of LLMs and discourse compared to prior narrowly scoped studies. It highlights a problem, offers a valuable solution (the unified labels), performs rigorous analysis and presents clear results. This isn't groundbreaking enough for a score above 8.5, and isn't merely incremental, so it receives this score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### PiSA: A Self-Augmented Data Engine and Training Strategy for 3D Understanding with Large Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10529v1)
- **Authors**: Zilu Guo, Hongbin Lin, Zhihao Yuan, Chaoda Zheng, Pengshuo Qiu, Dongzhi Jiang, Renrui Zhang, Chun-Mei Feng, Zhen Li
- **Abstract**: 3D Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have recently made substantial advancements. However, their potential remains untapped, primarily due to the limited quantity and suboptimal quality of 3D datasets. Current approaches attempt to transfer knowledge from 2D MLLMs to expand 3D instruction data, but still face modality and domain gaps. To this end, we introduce PiSA-Engine (Point-Self-Augmented-Engine), a new framework for generating instruction point-language datasets enriched with 3D spatial semantics. We observe that existing 3D MLLMs offer a comprehensive understanding of point clouds for annotation, while 2D MLLMs excel at cross-validation by providing complementary information. By integrating holistic 2D and 3D insights from off-the-shelf MLLMs, PiSA-Engine enables a continuous cycle of high-quality data generation. We select PointLLM as the baseline and adopt this co-evolution training framework to develop an enhanced 3D MLLM, termed PointLLM-PiSA. Additionally, we identify limitations in previous 3D benchmarks, which often feature coarse language captions and insufficient category diversity, resulting in inaccurate evaluations. To address this gap, we further introduce PiSA-Bench, a comprehensive 3D benchmark covering six key aspects with detailed and diverse labels. Experimental results demonstrate PointLLM-PiSA's state-of-the-art performance in zero-shot 3D object captioning and generative classification on our PiSA-Bench, achieving significant improvements of 46.45% (+8.33%) and 63.75% (+16.25%), respectively. We will release the code, datasets, and benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PiSA: A Self-Augmented Data Engine and Training Strategy for 3D Understanding with Large Models" addresses the limitations of 3D Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) related to insufficient and low-quality training data.  The authors introduce PiSA-Engine, a framework that leverages both 3D and 2D MLLMs to generate high-quality, spatially enriched instruction datasets. The engine consists of three stages: 3D-space data annotation (using 3D MLLMs), 2D-space data refinement (using 2D MLLMs for cross-validation), and iterative 3D data bootstrapping (co-evolution training).  They use PointLLM as a baseline, creating PointLLM-PiSA and introduce a new comprehensive 3D benchmark, PiSA-Bench, to evaluate performance across six aspects.  Experiments demonstrate improved zero-shot 3D object captioning and classification results using PointLLM-PiSA, validated by the PiSA-Bench benchmark. The authors claim to release their code, datasets, and benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of a self-augmented data engine for 3D understanding is valuable, but it borrows heavily from similar techniques used in 2D MLLM research. The novelty lies in adapting these concepts to the 3D domain and combining 3D and 2D MLLMs in a specific pipeline. The iterative data bootstrapping strategy is not groundbreaking, but the combination of the components and the design of the PiSA-Engine framework constitute a moderately novel contribution. The PiSA-Bench benchmark is a useful resource, addressing some limitations of existing 3D benchmarks by providing comprehensive evaluation across multiple aspects (description, color, shape, count, spatial relations, and usage).

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical bottleneck in the development of 3D MLLMs: the lack of high-quality training data. By providing an automatic data generation and iterative training strategy, PiSA-Engine can potentially accelerate progress in the field. The improvements reported on the proposed PiSA-Bench benchmark are significant, showcasing the effectiveness of the framework. The PiSA-Bench contributes by offering a detailed benchmark which attempts to comprehensively evaluate 3D MLLM. However, the reliance on GPT-4 for generating the benchmark data and evaluating the models introduces potential biases, which is acknowledged. This bias undermines the overall benchmark.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The PiSA-Engine framework is well-structured and integrates complementary strengths of 3D and 2D MLLMs.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate substantial performance gains using the proposed approach.
    *   The creation and release of PiSA-Bench address the lack of comprehensive evaluation benchmarks in the 3D domain.
    *   The iterative training strategy can be applied as a plug-and-play framework with other 3D MLLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The approach relies heavily on existing, potentially proprietary, LLMs (PointLLM and Qwen2-VL, and GPT-4o for evaluation), which limits accessibility and reproducibility. Specifically, for PiSA-Engine itself requires access to strong LLMs and it limits its usability in settings which cannot rely on these proprietary LLMs.
    *   The reliance on GPT-4 for evaluation introduces bias into the results. The evaluation strategy seems to heavily relies on GPT-4o and is limited given the bias. The improvement reported in the paper should be taken with caution.
    *   The use of 2D renderings introduces approximations of spatial information that can compromise data quality. The improvement in the captioning is limited by the capacity of LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The PiSA-Engine framework and PiSA-Bench benchmark have the potential to influence the 3D MLLM research community by enabling more efficient training and more comprehensive evaluation. However, the reliance on proprietary LLMs can limit the impact and adoption of the framework.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a useful engineering approach with moderate novelty and significance. While it effectively adapts existing techniques to the 3D domain and addresses the important problem of data scarcity, its reliance on proprietary LLMs and the potential for bias in the evaluation strategy limit its overall impact.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### KUDA: Keypoints to Unify Dynamics Learning and Visual Prompting for Open-Vocabulary Robotic Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10546v1)
- **Authors**: Zixian Liu, Mingtong Zhang, Yunzhu Li
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancement of large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs), significant progress has been made in developing open-vocabulary robotic manipulation systems. However, many existing approaches overlook the importance of object dynamics, limiting their applicability to more complex, dynamic tasks. In this work, we introduce KUDA, an open-vocabulary manipulation system that integrates dynamics learning and visual prompting through keypoints, leveraging both VLMs and learning-based neural dynamics models. Our key insight is that a keypoint-based target specification is simultaneously interpretable by VLMs and can be efficiently translated into cost functions for model-based planning. Given language instructions and visual observations, KUDA first assigns keypoints to the RGB image and queries the VLM to generate target specifications. These abstract keypoint-based representations are then converted into cost functions, which are optimized using a learned dynamics model to produce robotic trajectories. We evaluate KUDA on a range of manipulation tasks, including free-form language instructions across diverse object categories, multi-object interactions, and deformable or granular objects, demonstrating the effectiveness of our framework. The project page is available at http://kuda-dynamics.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "KUDA: Keypoints to Unify Dynamics Learning and Visual Prompting for Open-Vocabulary Robotic Manipulation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KUDA, a novel open-vocabulary robotic manipulation system. KUDA integrates dynamics learning and visual prompting using keypoints as a unifying representation.  Given a language instruction and visual observation, KUDA samples keypoints, uses a Vision-Language Model (VLM) to generate a code specifying keypoint-based target specifications, and translates these into cost functions. These cost functions are then optimized using a learned dynamics model for model-based planning, allowing the robot to perform manipulation tasks across various object categories. The system utilizes a two-level closed-loop control mechanism for robust execution, addressing both target specification imperfections and execution errors.  A prompt retriever is used to select appropriate examples for in-context learning with the VLM. The authors evaluate their system on tasks involving diverse object categories like ropes, cubes, and granular objects.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The central idea of using keypoints to bridge the gap between VLMs and dynamics learning is novel.  While visual prompting and dynamics learning are independently established fields, combining them in this specific way, with keypoints as the intermediary, is a worthwhile contribution.  The prompt retrieval mechanism is a nice addition, aimed at improving the VLM's performance through context-aware example selection. It builds upon existing in-context learning techniques. However, individual components like VLMs, dynamics models, and even visual prompting, are not novel in themselves. The key novelty lies in the *integration* strategy.

* **Significance:**  The significance of this work stems from its potential to overcome the limitations of purely VLM-driven or purely dynamics-driven robotic manipulation systems.  By leveraging the flexibility of VLMs for task specification and the accuracy of dynamics models for planning, KUDA can potentially handle more complex and dynamic manipulation tasks compared to systems that rely solely on either approach. The ability to handle deformable objects and granular materials, often challenging for other systems, further highlights its potential impact. The code and project page availability are a positive aspect promoting reproducibility and wider adoption.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Unified Representation:** The keypoint-based representation provides a neat way to connect the abstract language understanding capabilities of VLMs with the concrete physics simulations of dynamics models.
    * **Open-Vocabulary Manipulation:** The ability to handle free-form language instructions and diverse object categories is a valuable strength, addressing a key challenge in robotic manipulation.
    * **Closed-Loop Control:** The two-level closed-loop control mechanism increases the system's robustness and ability to recover from errors, an important feature for real-world deployment.
    * **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough empirical evaluation across a range of manipulation tasks, comparing KUDA to existing baselines and demonstrating its superior performance. The detailed error analysis is also beneficial, providing insights into the system's limitations and potential areas for improvement.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on VLM Performance:**  The system's performance is ultimately limited by the accuracy and robustness of the underlying VLM. If the VLM fails to generate accurate target specifications, the entire system will suffer.  The paper acknowledges this limitation, but the dependence is still a significant factor.
    * **Sim-to-Real Gap:** The dynamics models are trained in simulation, which can lead to discrepancies between simulated and real-world behavior. While the two-level closed-loop system addresses this to some extent, it's still a potential source of error.
    * **Top Camera Limitations:** The use of a top-down camera limits the ability to deal with more complex 3D manipulation tasks and occlusions.
    * **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** The individual components (VLM, dynamics model) are not groundbreaking. The innovation primarily lies in the architecture and integration strategy.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of robotic manipulation by demonstrating the benefits of combining VLMs and dynamics learning.  The keypoint-based representation could serve as a valuable starting point for future research in this area.  The system's ability to handle deformable objects and granular materials could open up new possibilities for robotic manipulation in various applications.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty and significance, balanced against the weaknesses discussed above, the paper represents a *solid* contribution to the field of robotic manipulation. While the individual components aren't entirely novel, the integration strategy is well-executed, and the empirical results are compelling. The limitations are clearly acknowledged. A higher score would have required a more fundamental breakthrough or a more complete solution to the sim-to-real gap. However, the presented work is a valuable step toward more capable and versatile robotic manipulation systems.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Short-term AI literacy intervention does not reduce over-reliance on incorrect ChatGPT recommendations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10556v1)
- **Authors**: Brett Puppart, Jaan Aru
- **Abstract**: In this study, we examined whether a short-form AI literacy intervention could reduce the adoption of incorrect recommendations from large language models. High school seniors were randomly assigned to either a control or an intervention group, which received an educational text explaining ChatGPT's working mechanism, limitations, and proper use. Participants solved math puzzles with the help of ChatGPT's recommendations, which were incorrect in half of the cases. Results showed that students adopted incorrect suggestions 52.1% of the time, indicating widespread over-reliance. The educational intervention did not significantly reduce over-reliance. Instead, it led to an increase in ignoring ChatGPT's correct recommendations. We conclude that the usage of ChatGPT is associated with over-reliance and it is not trivial to increase AI literacy to counter over-reliance.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary of the paper and a critical evaluation, including a score.

**Summary:**

This study investigates whether a short educational intervention on AI literacy can reduce over-reliance on incorrect recommendations from ChatGPT among high school seniors. Participants were randomly assigned to a control or an intervention group, with the latter receiving a text explaining ChatGPT's workings, limitations, and proper use. All participants then solved math puzzles with ChatGPT's assistance, where half of the AI's recommendations were intentionally incorrect. The results showed high rates of over-reliance, with students accepting incorrect suggestions over half the time. Surprisingly, the AI literacy intervention did not significantly reduce over-reliance. Instead, it led to an increased tendency to ignore correct ChatGPT recommendations. The authors conclude that ChatGPT use is associated with over-reliance, and improving AI literacy is not a straightforward solution to counteract this issue.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and timely issue: the potential for over-reliance on AI, specifically ChatGPT, in educational settings. The study has several strengths, including:

*   **Clear Research Question:** The research question is well-defined and directly relevant to ongoing discussions about AI in education.
*   **Experimental Design:** The use of a control group and an intervention group allows for a comparison to assess the impact of the AI literacy intervention.
*   **Appropriate Target Population:** Focusing on high school seniors is relevant, given the increasing integration of AI tools into secondary education.
*   **Interesting Findings:** The finding that the intervention did *not* reduce over-reliance, and *increased* under-reliance, is counterintuitive and warrants further investigation.

However, the paper also has weaknesses:

*   **Small Sample Size:** A final sample size of 36 participants is quite small, limiting the statistical power and generalizability of the findings. While the authors address this limitation in the discussion, it remains a significant concern.
*   **Limited Intervention:** The AI literacy intervention consisted of a single educational text. While the authors take inspiration from prior art, the scope of the intervention is rather narrow. A more multi-faceted intervention (e.g., including interactive exercises or discussion) might have been more effective.
*   **Task Domain:** The use of math puzzles, while offering a clear "right" or "wrong" answer, may not fully capture the nuances of over-reliance in other educational contexts (e.g., essay writing, research projects). Over-reliance might manifest differently in domains with more subjective assessments.
*   **Lack of a Follow-up Assessment:** The study does not assess the long-term impact of the intervention, which would have provided more robust insights into whether the induced 'AI literacy' led to behavioral changes over time.
* **Limited metrics:** Apart from the final answer, few other qualitative or other metrics were measured. This limits the type of conclusions that can be drawn.

**Novelty and Significance:**

While other studies have investigated over-reliance on AI, this paper adds to the literature by:

*   Specifically focusing on ChatGPT rather than more generic AI systems, which is important given ChatGPT's unique characteristics.
*   Examining over-reliance among high school students, a less studied population compared to university students.
*   Highlighting the potential *negative* impact of AI literacy interventions on correct recommendation use.

However, the small sample size and the relatively simple intervention somewhat limit the overall impact of the study. The result that the intervention may have reduced *reliance* overall, as opposed to *over-reliance* specifically may not be intuitive or convincing, without more convincing metrics to support the explanation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a timely and relevant study, and the counterintuitive findings regarding the AI literacy intervention are particularly noteworthy. However, the small sample size and narrow scope of the intervention limit the strength of the conclusions. Given the above, I assign a score of:

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 6/10

### ASIDE: Architectural Separation of Instructions and Data in Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10566v1)
- **Authors**: Egor Zverev, Evgenii Kortukov, Alexander Panfilov, Soroush Tabesh, Alexandra Volkova, Sebastian Lapuschkin, Wojciech Samek, Christoph H. Lampert
- **Abstract**: Despite their remarkable performance, large language models lack elementary safety features, and this makes them susceptible to numerous malicious attacks. In particular, previous work has identified the absence of an intrinsic separation between instructions and data as a root cause for the success of prompt injection attacks. In this work, we propose an architectural change, ASIDE, that allows the model to clearly separate between instructions and data by using separate embeddings for them. Instead of training the embeddings from scratch, we propose a method to convert an existing model to ASIDE form by using two copies of the original model's embeddings layer, and applying an orthogonal rotation to one of them. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our method by showing (1) highly increased instruction-data separation scores without a loss in model capabilities and (2) competitive results on prompt injection benchmarks, even without dedicated safety training. Additionally, we study the working mechanism behind our method through an analysis of model representations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ASIDE paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ASIDE (Architecturally Separated Instruction-Data Embeddings), a novel architectural modification for large language models (LLMs) designed to enhance the separation between instructions and data. Recognizing that the lack of intrinsic separation is a vulnerability exploited by prompt injection attacks, ASIDE uses separate embeddings for instructions and data.  Instead of training the embeddings from scratch, the paper proposes initializing these separate embeddings in existing models by copying the original embedding layer and applying an orthogonal rotation to one copy. The authors demonstrate, through experiments, that ASIDE significantly increases instruction-data separation scores without sacrificing model capabilities and achieves competitive results on prompt injection benchmarks, even without explicit safety training.  They also analyze model representations to understand the mechanisms behind ASIDE's effectiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of enforcing architectural separation between instructions and data within LLMs is a significant step beyond solely relying on prompting techniques or fine-tuning to mitigate prompt injection vulnerabilities. Previous research, like Zverev et al. (2025), highlighted the problem, but this work proposes a concrete *architectural* solution. The approach to integrate ASIDE into pre-trained models by orthogonal rotation of embeddings is clever and practical, minimizing retraining overhead. While concurrent work such as Wu et al (2024) introducing ISE shares some similarities in concept (role specific offsets), the more flexible token-specific embeddings of ASIDE differentiates it.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical security concern for LLMs, especially as they are increasingly integrated into larger software systems where the distinction between instructions and data is crucial. The demonstrated improvement in instruction-data separation and robustness against prompt injection attacks suggests that ASIDE could be a valuable building block for developing safer and more reliable LLMs. Even without explicit safety training, ASIDE showed promise, indicating a robust architectural advantage. The interpretability analysis, exploring how ASIDE influences the activation of instruction-related features, provides valuable insights into its working mechanism and could inspire further research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of insufficient instruction-data separation in current LLMs and its consequences.
    *   **Concrete Solution:** ASIDE offers a practical and relatively straightforward architectural modification.
    *   **Effective Methodology:** The experimental setup is well-designed, utilizing standard benchmarks and comparing ASIDE against strong baselines, including models using other proposed methods.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:**  The inclusion of model representation analysis, linear probing, and ablation studies strengthens the understanding of ASIDE's effectiveness and informs future research directions.
    *   **Integration into existing models**: the integration approach into already existing models with minor overhead and training makes the approach very usable.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Attack Scenarios:** While the paper demonstrates improved robustness against specific prompt injection attacks, it would be valuable to evaluate ASIDE against a broader range of attacks, including more sophisticated or adaptive strategies. The robustness gains on the Comp attack are more convincing than the gains on the naive attack.
    *   **Ablation of Design Choices could be more complete:** While the ablation study of ASIDE-Copy is valuable, exploring the impact of different rotation angles beyond just 90/270 degrees, or more sophisticated initialization schemes, could further refine the design.
    *   **Fine-tuning limitations**: The results are limited by the choice of the benign dataset in finetuning the ASIDE models.

*   **Potential Influence:** The ASIDE architecture has the potential to significantly impact the development of safer LLMs. It provides a starting point for more sophisticated architectural defenses against prompt injection attacks and could encourage further research into embedding-level techniques for enhancing LLM security. Further investigation into different variations and integration with safety-specific training methods could lead to even more robust solutions.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel, well-defined, and demonstrably effective architectural enhancement for LLMs to address a critical security vulnerability. The method is practical, and the supporting experiments and analyses are strong. While limitations exist regarding the breadth of attack scenarios tested and the depth of the ablation study, the overall contribution is significant and has the potential to influence future research and development in the field of LLM safety and security.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Autoregressive Image Generation with Randomized Parallel Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10568v1)
- **Authors**: Haopeng Li, Jinyue Yang, Guoqi Li, Huan Wang
- **Abstract**: We introduce ARPG, a novel visual autoregressive model that enables randomized parallel generation, addressing the inherent limitations of conventional raster-order approaches, which hinder inference efficiency and zero-shot generalization due to their sequential, predefined token generation order. Our key insight is that effective random-order modeling necessitates explicit guidance for determining the position of the next predicted token. To this end, we propose a novel guided decoding framework that decouples positional guidance from content representation, encoding them separately as queries and key-value pairs. By directly incorporating this guidance into the causal attention mechanism, our approach enables fully random-order training and generation, eliminating the need for bidirectional attention. Consequently, ARPG readily generalizes to zero-shot tasks such as image inpainting, outpainting, and resolution expansion. Furthermore, it supports parallel inference by concurrently processing multiple queries using a shared KV cache. On the ImageNet-1K 256 benchmark, our approach attains an FID of 1.94 with only 64 sampling steps, achieving over a 20-fold increase in throughput while reducing memory consumption by over 75% compared to representative recent autoregressive models at a similar scale.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Autoregressive Image Generation with Randomized Parallel Decoding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ARPG (Autoregressive Image Generation with Randomized Parallel Decoding), a novel visual autoregressive model designed to overcome limitations inherent in traditional raster-order AR models for image generation. The core idea is to enable training and inference in fully random token orders, addressing inefficiency and poor generalization. ARPG achieves this through a "guided decoding" framework where positional information is decoupled from content representation and encoded separately as queries and key-value pairs. This enables causal attention within the random order while also preserving the ability to leverage KV caches for efficient parallel decoding.  The paper demonstrates ARPG's effectiveness in class-conditional image generation, controllable generation (using canny edges and depth maps), and zero-shot generalization tasks like inpainting, outpainting, and resolution expansion.  The experiments show competitive image quality with significant gains in throughput and memory efficiency compared to existing autoregressive models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the "guided decoding" framework.  While random-order generation has been explored before (e.g., MaskGIT, RandAR), this work proposes a mechanism to enable *fully* random token order generation while maintaining causality (crucial for autoregressive modeling) *and* enabling efficient parallel decoding via KV caching.  Decoupling positional guidance is a significant improvement, particularly compared to RandAR's approach of injecting positional tokens, which increases sequence length and computational cost.

*   **Significance:** The significance is high because ARPG directly addresses two critical limitations of AR image generation: inference speed and generalization capabilities. The impressive gains in throughput (reported as a 20-fold increase) and memory efficiency make high-resolution AR image generation more practical.  The ability to perform zero-shot tasks like inpainting and outpainting natively, without architectural modifications or specialized training, is also a substantial advantage. The approach also opens possibilities of exploring other controllable generation tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Technically Sound:** The "guided decoding" framework is well-explained and seems logically consistent with the overall goal.
    *   **Significant Efficiency Gains:** The reported throughput and memory efficiency improvements are substantial and well-quantified.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of ARPG across a range of tasks and demonstrate competitive quality to other AR models.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is clearly written and explains the approach effectively.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** While the core idea is understandable, the technical implementation details (especially regarding RoPE manipulation and decoder architecture) could be more thoroughly explained, particularly in supplementary material.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** While the paper compares against several AR models, including diffusion-based models could have strengthened the analysis, especially with respect to image quality.
    *   **Tokenizer Dependence:** Reliance on a specific tokenizer could limit applicability of this model and might introduce dependence on the effectiveness of the pre-trained tokenizer.

*   **Potential Impact:** ARPG has the potential to significantly influence the field of autoregressive image generation. The gains in efficiency could make AR models a more viable option for high-resolution image synthesis compared to other methods. The zero-shot generalization capabilities and its applicability to controllable generation open up new applications that were previously difficult with vanilla AR models.

*   **Comparison to Similar Approaches:** The paper adequately compares to RandAR, MaskGIT, and other common AR models, highlighting the trade-offs in memory, computational overhead, and inference speed. However, a deeper discussion around how the random token order impacts perceived image quality or style compared to standard raster scans could benefit the paper.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

ARPG represents a significant advance in autoregressive image generation. The "guided decoding" framework is a novel and technically sound solution that effectively tackles limitations in previous methods. While a deeper dive into the tokenizer's influence and additional comparisons to diffusion methods could improve the analysis, the demonstrated efficiency gains, zero-shot generalization capabilities, and applicability for controllable generation tasks are substantial enough to warrant a high score. ARPG has a clear potential to shape future research directions in AR image generation.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Radar: Fast Long-Context Decoding for Any Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10571v1)
- **Authors**: Yongchang Hao, Mengyao Zhai, Hossein Hajimirsadeghi, Sepidehsadat Hosseini, Frederick Tung
- **Abstract**: Transformer models have demonstrated exceptional performance across a wide range of applications. Though forming the foundation of Transformer models, the dot-product attention does not scale well to long-context data since its time requirement grows quadratically with context length. In this work, we propose Radar, a training-free approach that accelerates inference by dynamically searching for the most important context tokens. For any pre-trained Transformer, Radar can reduce the decoding time complexity without training or heuristically evicting tokens. Moreover, we provide theoretical justification for our approach, demonstrating that Radar can reliably identify the most important tokens with high probability. We conduct extensive comparisons with the previous methods on a wide range of tasks. The results demonstrate that Radar achieves the state-of-the-art performance across different architectures with reduced time complexity, offering a practical solution for efficient long-context processing of Transformers.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RADAR (Range search Accelerated by Random features), a training-free approach for accelerating inference with Transformer models on long-context data. RADAR dynamically selects the most important context tokens by grouping the context into segments and using random projections to maintain a summarization representation for each segment.  The importance of each group is calculated, and only tokens from the most important segments are used for generating new tokens.  The authors provide a theoretical justification for RADAR, demonstrating its ability to identify important tokens with high probability. Experiments across various tasks and models are presented, showing that RADAR achieves state-of-the-art performance with reduced time complexity compared to existing methods. The code is made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its training-free, dynamic token selection approach. Unlike methods that require retraining or those that simply evict tokens based on heuristics (e.g., StreamingLLM), RADAR offers a more principled way to reduce computational complexity while preserving relevant contextual information. The use of random projections for efficient segment summarization is also a notable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The problem of scaling Transformers to long contexts is a crucial challenge in NLP and other fields. RADAR provides a practical solution that can be readily applied to pre-trained models without the need for fine-tuning. The reported performance gains and reduced time complexity make it a significant contribution to efficient Transformer inference. The theoretical guarantees further enhance the significance of the work by providing a strong foundation for the approach.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Training-free: RADAR is easily applicable to existing pre-trained models.
    *   Dynamic token selection:  Selects the most relevant tokens rather than blindly evicting them.
    *   Theoretical guarantees: Provides a rigorous justification for the approach.
    *   Strong empirical results: Demonstrates state-of-the-art performance across various tasks and models.
    *   Publicly available code: Facilitates reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The complexity analysis, while showing a reduction, still involves O(t^1.5) complexity which isn't linear. While better than quadratic, further optimization might be possible.
    *   The paper mentions that memory usage is slightly higher than existing methods due to maintaining segment representations. This could be a concern for resource-constrained environments, even if it scales sublinearly. Future work on memory optimization is recognized, but it does temper the current implementation.
    *   Although the results are impressive, some comparisons in the appendix show failures of Radar on other model types. Further discussion of this situation and how the technique could be made more robust is warranted.

*   **Potential Influence:** RADAR has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and theoretically sound method for efficient long-context processing with Transformers. It could be used to accelerate inference in various applications, such as document summarization, question answering, and code generation. It could also inspire further research on dynamic token selection and efficient attention mechanisms.

*   **Justification for Score:**  The paper makes a significant contribution to improving the efficiency of transformer models for long-context tasks by providing a novel, training-free, and theoretically grounded method. The empirical results support the claims of state-of-the-art performance with reduced computational cost. While the memory overhead and room for further optimization are limitations, the overall impact and novelty of the approach warrant a positive evaluation.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Unveiling the Mathematical Reasoning in DeepSeek Models: A Comparative Study of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10573v1)
- **Authors**: Afrar Jahin, Arif Hassan Zidan, Yu Bao, Shizhe Liang, Tianming Liu, Wei Zhang
- **Abstract**: With the rapid evolution of Artificial Intelligence (AI), Large Language Models (LLMs) have reshaped the frontiers of various fields, spanning healthcare, public health, engineering, science, agriculture, education, arts, humanities, and mathematical reasoning. Among these advancements, DeepSeek models have emerged as noteworthy contenders, demonstrating promising capabilities that set them apart from their peers. While previous studies have conducted comparative analyses of LLMs, few have delivered a comprehensive evaluation of mathematical reasoning across a broad spectrum of LLMs. In this work, we aim to bridge this gap by conducting an in-depth comparative study, focusing on the strengths and limitations of DeepSeek models in relation to their leading counterparts. In particular, our study systematically evaluates the mathematical reasoning performance of two DeepSeek models alongside five prominent LLMs across three independent benchmark datasets. The findings reveal several key insights: 1). DeepSeek-R1 consistently achieved the highest accuracy on two of the three datasets, demonstrating strong mathematical reasoning capabilities. 2). The distilled variant of LLMs significantly underperformed compared to its peers, highlighting potential drawbacks in using distillation techniques. 3). In terms of response time, Gemini 2.0 Flash demonstrated the fastest processing speed, outperforming other models in efficiency, which is a crucial factor for real-time applications. Beyond these quantitative assessments, we delve into how architecture, training, and optimization impact LLMs' mathematical reasoning. Moreover, our study goes beyond mere performance comparison by identifying key areas for future advancements in LLM-driven mathematical reasoning. This research enhances our understanding of LLMs' mathematical reasoning and lays the groundwork for future advancements
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comparative study of the mathematical reasoning capabilities of several Large Language Models (LLMs), with a particular focus on DeepSeek models. The authors evaluate two DeepSeek variants (R1 and a distilled version) alongside prominent LLMs like Google's Gemini 2.0 Flash, OpenAI's GPT-4o and earlier models, across three benchmark datasets (MATH, GSM8K, and MMLU). The study analyzes accuracy, response time, and performance variations across different mathematical domains (e.g., abstract algebra, formal logic). The findings highlight DeepSeek-R1's strong performance in structured problem-solving but also identify limitations in abstract mathematical areas. The paper also investigates the impact of model distillation on reasoning ability and discusses the implications of using Reinforcement Learning (RL) in training LLMs for mathematical tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper offers a reasonably thorough comparative analysis, evaluating several LLMs on multiple datasets and metrics. This broad scope provides a more nuanced understanding of their mathematical reasoning capabilities than studies focusing on a single model or dataset.
    *   **Focus on DeepSeek:** The focus on DeepSeek models, especially DeepSeek-R1, is justified by their recent emergence and potential as strong contenders in mathematical reasoning.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The paper identifies key factors influencing performance, such as the impact of model distillation and the effectiveness of RL-based training (GRPO). The investigation into different mathematical domains (abstract algebra, etc.) highlights specific strengths and weaknesses of various LLMs.
    *   **Good Datasets Selection:** The datasets selection (MATH, GSM8K, MMLU) provide diversity and appropriate levels of difficulty.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Scope:** While the paper includes several prominent models, it acknowledges the omission of other recent and powerful LLMs such as Llama-3.x, Mistral, and Claude 3. Expanding the model scope would enhance the comprehensiveness and impact of the study.
    *   **Zero-Shot Prompting Only:** The use of zero-shot prompting provides a consistent evaluation baseline but doesn't explore the potential benefits of few-shot learning or chain-of-thought prompting strategies. Investigating different prompting methods could reveal valuable insights into how to elicit better mathematical reasoning from these models.
    *   **Lack of Novel Benchmarking Contributions:** The study doesn't introduce any new benchmarks or methodologies for evaluating mathematical reasoning. It relies on existing datasets and metrics, which, while well-established, may not fully capture the nuances of complex mathematical problem-solving.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While figures highlight certain strengths or weaknesses qualitatively, there's limited exploration in *how* specific models approach problems. For instance, error analyses are lacking and could contribute significantly to the analysis.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's significance lies in its comprehensive comparative analysis and its detailed examination of DeepSeek models' mathematical capabilities. It contributes to the growing body of literature evaluating LLMs for complex reasoning tasks.
    *   However, the novelty is somewhat limited. The study does not introduce substantially new methodologies or findings. While the focus on DeepSeek is valuable, the conclusions largely confirm existing knowledge about the impact of distillation and RL-based training.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could influence future research by providing a benchmark for evaluating new LLMs and by highlighting the strengths and limitations of different training approaches. The insights into DeepSeek models could stimulate further research on RL-based training and its application to mathematical reasoning.
    *   The analysis of model performance across different mathematical domains may guide the development of specialized LLMs tailored to specific problem-solving areas.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, and the limited novelty, a score of 6/10 is assigned. The paper offers a solid comparative evaluation but falls short of making a truly groundbreaking contribution to the field. While it expands upon existing knowledge, the lack of novel benchmarking approaches, limited model scope, and limited error analyses, and other methodological considerations temper its overall impact.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Unlock the Power of Unlabeled Data in Language Driving Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10586v1)
- **Authors**: Chaoqun Wang, Jie Yang, Xiaobin Hong, Ruimao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent Vision-based Large Language Models~(VisionLLMs) for autonomous driving have seen rapid advancements. However, such promotion is extremely dependent on large-scale high-quality annotated data, which is costly and labor-intensive. To address this issue, we propose unlocking the value of abundant yet unlabeled data to improve the language-driving model in a semi-supervised learning manner. Specifically, we first introduce a series of template-based prompts to extract scene information, generating questions that create pseudo-answers for the unlabeled data based on a model trained with limited labeled data. Next, we propose a Self-Consistency Refinement method to improve the quality of these pseudo-annotations, which are later used for further training. By utilizing a pre-trained VisionLLM (e.g., InternVL), we build a strong Language Driving Model (LDM) for driving scene question-answering, outperforming previous state-of-the-art methods. Extensive experiments on the DriveLM benchmark show that our approach performs well with just 5% labeled data, achieving competitive performance against models trained with full datasets. In particular, our LDM achieves 44.85% performance with limited labeled data, increasing to 54.27% when using unlabeled data, while models trained with full datasets reach 60.68% on the DriveLM benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of training language-driven models (LDMs) for autonomous driving with limited labeled data. It proposes a semi-supervised learning approach to unlock the potential of abundant unlabeled data. The method involves generating pseudo-labels for unlabeled data using template-based prompts and refining these pseudo-labels using a Self-Consistency Refinement (SCR) technique. SCR leverages scene graph information to provide contextual hints, improving the quality and reliability of pseudo-annotations. The refined pseudo-labeled data is then used to iteratively train the LDM. Experiments on the DriveLM benchmark demonstrate that the proposed method achieves competitive performance with just a small fraction of labeled data compared to models trained on full datasets. The paper claims a novel approach to leveraging unlabeled data for LDM training, presenting SCR as a key component for improving pseudo-label quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of template-based prompt generation, scene graph construction for contextual hints, and self-consistency refinement is a reasonably novel contribution.  While individual components have been explored previously in other contexts, their integration within the LDM training framework for autonomous driving is a unique aspect of this work. The use of scene graphs for providing hints to improve pseudo-label quality appears promising.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in reducing the dependence on large, expensive, and time-consuming labeled datasets for training LDMs in autonomous driving. If the proposed approach consistently delivers strong performance with limited labeling, it can have a practical impact on reducing annotation costs and accelerating model development. The potential for continuous improvement through iterative training with unlabeled data is also a valuable aspect.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper addresses a relevant and crucial problem in autonomous driving: the scarcity of labeled data.
    *   The proposed SCR method with graph-based hints provides a structured way to improve pseudo-label quality, which is often a major bottleneck in semi-supervised learning.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate significant performance gains compared to baselines with limited labeled data, and even approach the performance of fully supervised models.
    *   The approach is conceptually clear and well-motivated.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dependence on manually designed templates for question generation and scene graph construction is a potential limitation. The effectiveness of the approach likely depends on the quality and coverage of these templates. The robustness of the templates across diverse and challenging driving scenarios is unclear.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the failure cases and limitations of the SCR method. It's important to understand the scenarios where the self-consistency check fails to identify incorrect pseudo-labels.
    *   The results are primarily focused on the DriveLM benchmark.  While this is a standard dataset, demonstrating the generalizability of the approach to other driving datasets or real-world data would strengthen the paper's impact.
    *   The paper could delve deeper into the computational complexity of the scene graph construction and SCR methods, particularly concerning scalability to large datasets.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence future research on semi-supervised learning for autonomous driving. The idea of leveraging scene graph information to improve pseudo-label quality is a valuable insight that can be further explored and extended. It could potentially encourage research on more automated and robust methods for question generation and scene graph construction.

*   **Justification for the Score:** The paper presents a moderately novel and valuable approach to a significant problem in autonomous driving. The strengths of the SCR method and the promising experimental results justify a positive evaluation. However, the limitations related to template design, failure case analysis, and generalizability prevent it from being considered a truly exceptional contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Long Context Tuning for Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10589v1)
- **Authors**: Yuwei Guo, Ceyuan Yang, Ziyan Yang, Zhibei Ma, Zhijie Lin, Zhenheng Yang, Dahua Lin, Lu Jiang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in video generation can produce realistic, minute-long single-shot videos with scalable diffusion transformers. However, real-world narrative videos require multi-shot scenes with visual and dynamic consistency across shots. In this work, we introduce Long Context Tuning (LCT), a training paradigm that expands the context window of pre-trained single-shot video diffusion models to learn scene-level consistency directly from data. Our method expands full attention mechanisms from individual shots to encompass all shots within a scene, incorporating interleaved 3D position embedding and an asynchronous noise strategy, enabling both joint and auto-regressive shot generation without additional parameters. Models with bidirectional attention after LCT can further be fine-tuned with context-causal attention, facilitating auto-regressive generation with efficient KV-cache. Experiments demonstrate single-shot models after LCT can produce coherent multi-shot scenes and exhibit emerging capabilities, including compositional generation and interactive shot extension, paving the way for more practical visual content creation. See https://guoyww.github.io/projects/long-context-video/ for more details.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Long Context Tuning for Video Generation" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Long Context Tuning (LCT), a training paradigm that extends the context window of pre-trained single-shot video diffusion models to generate coherent multi-shot scenes. LCT adapts the full attention mechanism to encompass all shots within a scene, incorporating an interleaved 3D position embedding and an asynchronous noise strategy.  This allows for both joint and auto-regressive shot generation without adding parameters. The authors demonstrate that single-shot models, after LCT, can produce coherent multi-shot scenes and exhibit capabilities like compositional generation and interactive shot extension.  A context-causal attention fine-tuning technique further enhances auto-regressive generation through efficient KV-cache usage.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of expanding the context window of existing single-shot video generation models for multi-shot consistency is relatively novel. The specific techniques, such as interleaved 3D positional embeddings and the asynchronous noise strategy, appear to be the more significant innovations. The context-causal attention fine-tuning is also a valuable optimization. The overall method builds upon existing DiT architectures, making it an incremental improvement rather than a complete paradigm shift. The overall effect provides a novel approach for video generation, which makes the paper highly interesting for researchers in the field.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in video generation: the ability to create long, coherent narrative videos with multiple shots. While single-shot generation has made considerable progress, generating videos with scene-level consistency remains a challenge. LCT offers a plausible approach to bridge this gap, leading to more practical visual content creation. The emergent capabilities, especially compositional generation and interactive shot extension, demonstrate the potential of the method.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-defined problem statement.
    *   Technically sound approach with a reasonable set of proposed solutions (interleaved embedding, asynchronous noise strategy, context-causal attention).
    *   Experiments demonstrate promising results, showcasing the ability to generate coherent multi-shot scenes and novel capabilities.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the effectiveness of different components.
    *   Qualitative results showcase clear improvement over baseline methods.
    * The LCT method is simple and efficient to implement on top of various existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The quantitative evaluation, while present, could be more comprehensive and robust. The dependence on VBench for shot-level quality is good, but dedicated scene-level consistency metrics would strengthen the claims. The user study, though included, lacks details on participants and study design, which could raise concerns.
    *   The paper relies heavily on qualitative comparisons, which are subjective.
    *   The reliance on Gemini-1.5 for captioning introduces potential biases.
    * The method is still quite limited and can't be easily applied to more complex scene setups.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in video generation by highlighting the importance of scene-level consistency and providing a practical method for achieving it. The idea of extending context windows and adapting existing single-shot models might inspire other researchers. The code availability can also increase the method's overall impact on the field.

*   **Overall Assessment:** While LCT is an incremental advancement rather than a revolutionary one, it provides a significant step toward scene-level video generation. The technical contributions are sound, and the results are promising. The limitations are well-acknowledged, and future work should address them, especially in more robust quantitative evaluation.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper makes a solid, novel contribution to a practically relevant problem. The significance lies in the ability to generate coherent multi-shot scenes and enable interactive video creation. The limitations in evaluation and potential biases detract somewhat from the score, but the overall approach is strong and impactful.

- **Classification**: cs.CV

### CameraCtrl II: Dynamic Scene Exploration via Camera-controlled Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10592v1)
- **Authors**: Hao He, Ceyuan Yang, Shanchuan Lin, Yinghao Xu, Meng Wei, Liangke Gui, Qi Zhao, Gordon Wetzstein, Lu Jiang, Hongsheng Li
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces CameraCtrl II, a framework that enables large-scale dynamic scene exploration through a camera-controlled video diffusion model. Previous camera-conditioned video generative models suffer from diminished video dynamics and limited range of viewpoints when generating videos with large camera movement. We take an approach that progressively expands the generation of dynamic scenes -- first enhancing dynamic content within individual video clip, then extending this capability to create seamless explorations across broad viewpoint ranges. Specifically, we construct a dataset featuring a large degree of dynamics with camera parameter annotations for training while designing a lightweight camera injection module and training scheme to preserve dynamics of the pretrained models. Building on these improved single-clip techniques, we enable extended scene exploration by allowing users to iteratively specify camera trajectories for generating coherent video sequences. Experiments across diverse scenarios demonstrate that CameraCtrl Ii enables camera-controlled dynamic scene synthesis with substantially wider spatial exploration than previous approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CameraCtrl II paper:

**Summary:**

The CameraCtrl II paper introduces a novel framework for dynamic scene exploration using camera-controlled video diffusion models. Building on previous work, it addresses limitations in existing models regarding dynamic content generation and spatial exploration range. The key contributions include: (1) a new dataset curated from dynamic videos with camera trajectory annotations derived using SfM; (2) a lightweight camera injection module and training scheme designed to preserve the dynamic capabilities of the pre-trained diffusion model; and (3) a clip-wise autoregressive generation technique for extended scene exploration by iteratively allowing users to specify camera trajectories. The method demonstrates superior performance in camera control accuracy, dynamic content generation, and spatial exploration compared to existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits several aspects of novelty. The curated dataset (REALCAM), designed specifically to address the limitations of existing datasets with respect to dynamics, is a significant contribution.  The lightweight camera injection module and training strategy, aiming to preserve the dynamic generation capability, presents a refined approach compared to more intrusive methods. The sequential video generation approach, coupled with relative pose calculations, enables broader scene exploration than single-clip methods.

*   **Significance:** The ability to explore dynamic scenes with user-defined camera control represents a significant advance. The paper addresses a key limitation in camera-controlled video diffusion models, paving the way for more interactive and immersive experiences. Overcoming the limitations of generating dynamic content with large camera movements expands the scope of applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The dataset curation methodology is well-explained and addresses a critical need for dynamic video datasets.
    *   The lightweight camera injection module is a clever design choice that addresses the issue of diminished dynamics.
    *   The clip-wise autoregressive approach tackles the problem of limited exploration range, a key bottleneck in previous works.
    *   The quantitative results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods across multiple metrics.
    *   Ablation studies rigorously validate the importance of each component of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper mentions certain limitations, such as occasional difficulties in resolving conflicts between camera movement and scene geometry.  Further research is needed to fully address these challenges, particularly concerning physical plausibility.
    *   While the performance improves the baselines, the improvement on appearance consistency metric is significant but remains relatively low when compared to its potential for improvement.
    *   The need for two-phase distillation is not ideal. A more streamlined single-phase process could improve user experience and reduce computational costs.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research on controllable video generation, dynamic scene exploration, and interactive content creation. The curated dataset could become a valuable resource for the community. The approach of preserving pretrained model capabilities while incorporating camera control is a promising direction. The exploration methodology opens up possibilities for generating long, coherent video sequences based on user input.

*   **Score Justification:**

While the paper offers substantial advancements, especially in camera control and scene exploration, it doesn't completely revolutionize the field. The limitations in resolving geometrical inconsistencies and potential for further improvements in visual consistency prevent it from achieving the highest score. It does, however, constitute a notable and impactful contribution that builds effectively on prior research, therefore a high score is justified.
